ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u4	Honourable President of the National Assembly, Secretary of State, Fellow Member and Fellow Members! Cancer, a disease of modern times, affects more than 12,000 Slovenes and Slovenians each year, regretting about half the battle with this disease. Cancer is a disease of long-lived society. Nearly 60% of the patients are over 60 years of age, and that is why we have about 2% more patients each year in Slovenia. There is virtually no one among us who does not know someone who has suffered cancer successfully; but unfortunately he also knows someone for whom the disease has been fatal. Timely detection of the presence of the disease remains crucial in the fight and recovery. With this, the disease never rests. Severe side effects: loss of appetite, vomiting, nausea, muscle spasms, personal distress are caused by prescribed strong medicines and therapies in many patients. Patients facing comparable symptoms also include Alzheimer’s and Parkinson’ s disease and a number of epileptics. These are estimated at more than 10 000, so we have tabled in the legislative process a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Production and Trafficking of illicit drugs. With it, we want to refer to an unsustainable state. Many of the most difficult patients are forced to serve the black market, thus endangering their health, while in their most difficult lifetime degrade themselves to the level of the offender and the criminal. The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the production and trafficking of illicit drugs, as proposed by us in the SAB Group, introduces and lays down conditions for the cultivation and processing of hemp for medical purposes, lays down more specifically the powers to issue authorisations for the production and marketing of hemp for medical purposes, and regulates fully the supervision of the activities concerned. For medical purposes, it is already possible to use synthetic products from natural hemp extracts for medical purposes, but Slovenia is mainly dependent on imports from abroad. Imported products available are only synthetic and their concentrations are significantly lower in order to achieve the desired values. They're hard to access and expensive. In Slovenia, we need to provide conditions for cultivation and thus allow patients access to a quality and affordable medicinal product from natural hemp. Since, as we say, we are only now bound by imports of extremely limited quantities, since only the use of medical hemp, not cultivation and processing, would be possible, the amendment would regulate the legislative procedures of medical hemp and thereby significantly improve the accessibility of the raw material to patients and, of course, improve the safety of its consumption. With the bill we refer only to a narrow area in which there is a very broad social consensus that it must be addressed and resolved. In the SAB party, we are aware that there is a desire for wider regulation of the area, which raises added issues and is more time-consuming and substantively difficult. In the past, the scope of cannabis has been repeatedly addressed, but unfortunately not successful. Unfortunately, from the discussions in the procedure so far, the SAB party is unable to remove the feeling that some parties are concerned instead of the patient's benefits, especially the issue of prestige. We stress that although we have sent the law for review, we have not received any comments on the article of the law despite promises. In doing so, we draw attention to the position of the Government, which does not support the law, as it prepares its better, systematic. The deadline for fulfilling these promises has passed for more than one month. We are aware and committed to regulating cannabis for medical purposes, with a view to all individuals and relatives who are currently forced to serve the black market and thus expose themselves not only to law enforcement authorities but also to some kind of production and an uncontrolled dose. We therefore appeal to all Members and Members in the SAB party as a proposal not to support the conclusion that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u5	Thank you very much. The position of the Government will be presented by Mrs Alenka Forte, State Secretary at the Ministry of Health.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u6	Mr President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In opening, let me point out that the proposal for a law tabled by a group of Members does not address the issue of cannabis cultivation for medical purposes in a comprehensive and clear manner. The proposed law would violate the provisions of the Single Convention on Drugs, which, under clearly defined conditions, allows signatory countries to grow cannabis for scientific, scientific and medical purposes. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia opposes the proposal of the law because it is inconsistent with concrete solutions. Let me introduce you to her. The proposal does not define the basic conditions for the cultivation, drying and testing of hemp for medical purposes. In technical and professional terms, the cultivation of medical hemp is therefore particularly difficult, as high quality standards of produced and dried plants depend on the safety, quality and efficiency of the products and medicines to be produced from it. It is not clear from the bill whether the production of medical hemp on six hectares is envisaged in open or closed areas or in greenhouses. It should be pointed out here that the appropriate quality and stability of crop production cannot be ensured in the fields. In addition, there is a high potential for outdoor cultivation of its own contamination and contamination of other plants and seeds in the environment. It should also be noted that the risk of depriving or stealing cannabis is also significantly higher in outdoor cultivation than in the case of cultivation in closed areas, which are adequately protected. The provisions of the proposed law on redemption, resale and storage are not in line with the Single Convention, and the provisions on the competence of the authorities to issue authorisations for the cultivation of hemp are inconsistent with both the Ministry of Health and the Public Agency for Medicinal Products and Medical Devices. The latter currently lacks neither the staffing nor the spatial conditions for the performance of the tasks of the authority concerned, and therefore the implementation of the law in this respect would constitute an increase in the public financial obligation. In particular, it should be pointed out that the proposal for a law does not contain criminal provisions, and the proposal also provides for a short period between the application of the law and the actual application of the law. We agree that in Slovenia we need an open and professional debate in which the views of the profession and all stakeholders are clearly presented. Today's and other debates contribute confidently to the fact that decisions on the cultivation of cannabis for medical purposes in Slovenia are based on international law and on professional and scientific grounds. Above all, we want to take into account the scientific knowledge and interest of people, especially those most vulnerable, both in today's debate and in decision-making, these are children, adolescents and patients. It should be noted that the non-critical promotion of cannabis as a versatilely effective plant poses a number of risks, as data from the annual report on the state of illicit drugs indicate that the number of cannabis poisonings in Slovenia is increasing annually, as well as a large proportion of those who abuse cannabis among young people. Compared to their peers in European countries, we far exceed this level of cannabis abuse. They are doctors and should remain independent and autonomous in the treatment of their patients. Doctors are aware that all medicines available can be used in the treatment of their patients, including canabinoids, of course, if this is mainly in accordance with professional recommendations or protocols for the treatment of disease. This is how canabinoids are used in the treatment of paediatric patients, neurologists and, above all, oncologists. Let me get this straight. The Ministry of Health shall carry out all the measures and activities necessary to ensure safe, effective and accessible medicines for patients required by medical indications, one of our most important tasks and responsibilities. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u13	Thank you very much. Andrej Černigoj will present the position of the Parliamentary Group Nova Slovenija – Christian Democrats.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u14	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Dear Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues! In the New Slovenia Group of Members – Christian Democrats, we are very careful about amending legislation on the production and transport of illicit drugs. On the one hand, we are examining all the arguments given, and on the other hand we do not want to promise citizens and citizens more than we recommend and allow international rules. The Single Convention of the United Nations on Drugs of 1961, which has also been adopted by Slovenia, allows for the cultivation of cannabis for medical purposes, provided that the country has strict control over its production, processing and transport. The establishment of a national authority responsible for controlling the cultivation of hemp is required and therefore the authorisation is granted, while at the same time it has the exclusive right to purchase and sell hemp. The competent authority must also have the exclusive right of import, export, wholesale trade and stock storage under the Convention. The authorisation for the cultivation of hemp may only allow growers to grow, harvest and sell crops to a national authority, any processing or extraction of hemp from it to a producer under the Convention. Legal access to cannabis products is possible in the Republic of Slovenia when this is medically justified. First, in 2014, the government made it possible to access synthetic cannabinoids, and in 2017 the classification of hemp plant, hemp resin and hemp extracts was changed, allowing for medical use also the flower and fertile hemp tips. The area that the proposed law touches only superficially is very difficult. Everything is not as simple as it seems at first sight, as we as a country are committed to respecting international agreements. In view of the scale of this black market, the international community can only work together to combat illicit trafficking in illicit drugs, not only us, but only together with other countries, because illicit trade does not refer to national borders. In the New Slovenia Group of Members – Christian Democrats, we are aware of the pace of life today and of all the diseases that this way of life brings. We live at a time when cancer in all its different forms becomes the greatest enemy of life that does not choose among the younger or older population. We are aware that cannabinoids in oncology can have palliative effects, domestically, are able to relieve the symptoms caused by cancer and chemotherapy. We feel sorry for anyone who is in a difficult situation because of a serious illness. We understand that medical hemp treatment can be medically justified in such cases. However, it should be borne in mind that the medical hemp was never the first choice in treatment, but is only one alternative when the first treatment does not help, or as a supportive treatment. Medical cannabis belongs to medicinal products and, as such, must be subject to very strict control as well as other medicinal products of this class. Today's proposal to amend the Act on the production and trafficking of illicit drugs, as tabled in the Parliamentary Procedure by the SAB Group, does not provide for this necessary control. We have not supported the proposed novels on the Home Committee of the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats. The failure to support the law by the three ministries, the Council of State and a number of concerns of the Legislative and Legal Service confirm that our decision was correct. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u20	The mother’s working body has decided that the proposal for a novel Law on the production and transport of illicit drugs, which would regulate the cultivation, production, processing and use of hemp for medical purposes, is inappropriate for further treatment. We regret the decision of the working body in the Group of Non-Related Members. We are aware that the Legislative and Legal Service has warned that the issue under consideration requires comprehensive regulation and that the proposed solutions are lacking. Nevertheless, in the Group of Members, we support all proposals to move forward in this area. The area of use of cannabis for medical purposes has already been regulated for the last two mandates, but we have maintained that, last time in 2017, we have made it possible to use mainly synthetic medicines containing hemp extracts. In 2017 we moved hemp from the first group of illicit drugs to the second group. In the first group, plants and substances — very dangerous to human health due to the severe consequences that their abuse can cause and are not used in medicine. In another group, however, there are plants and substances that are otherwise hazardous to health but can be used in medicine. Amendments to the Regulation on the classification of illicit drugs have made it possible to prescribe cannabinoids for medical purposes for medical purposes, but these medicines are only available to patients from imports, since cannabis production and processing for medical purposes have remained prohibited for us. In 2018, two different legislative proposals were thus formulated to address the issue of the use of cannabis for both recreational and medical purposes, but they did not wait for epilogues in the legislative process. The applicants sought to prevent the black market by regulation and to enable patients and other users in particular to reach the THC-rich hemp in a legal manner. Now they have to buy it on the black market, and that also means a lot of risk. The Health Committee has already adopted conclusions in 2018 calling on the Government to regulate this area, but there has not been an appropriate response to date. Similarly, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has to date failed to provide an appropriate Slovenian translation of the Single Convention on Drugs of 1961. Until there is an appropriate translation or, above all, an understanding of the concept of cannabis, regulation will be based on wrong assumptions. For many years, the need for radical and comprehensive amendment of cannabis legislation has been pointed out by informed and growing civil society, doctors and scientists. They support research and proper regulation of cannabis for medical purposes and point out that it is time to change the skeletal views on the use of this plant. Why? Due to its comprehensive use and positive effects and many opportunities for an economy facing many problems during this difficult period. The fact is that Slovenian legislation in the field of cannabis is one of the most outdated. In the European Union, cannabis is a multibillion-dollar business, which is of no benefit to industry but also to medicine. In 2020, the European Medical Hemp Market was worth 250 million euros. In the following year, the value is expected to double. Experts estimate that by 2024 the legal market for cannabis in Europe will be up to 2.1 billion euros, so more than 40 countries worldwide have legalised medical hemp. Currently, in 19 countries across Europe, the use of medical cannabis is legal if prescribed by a doctor. Prohibition and inadequate regulation do the most harm to whom? Hospitals. Therefore, bolder steps are urgently needed. The potential of medical cannabis in health care is already recognised for certain diseases and is subject to research for many others. That is why this area represents a remarkable potential and a large market. Slovenia has the knowledge that companies dealing with medical hemp are expecting only the preparation of appropriate legal bases and support for development needed for a competitive performance in the European and global markets. As you know, the regulation of legislation in this area has been a priority for us and has also been included in the coalition contract of the current government at my initiative. Despite the fact that I am no longer a member of the government coalition, we will continue as unrelated Members with our colleagues in the effort to draft the law on the use of cannabis.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u28	Thank you for your word. Once again a lovely greeting, the distinguished President of the National Assembly, the honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The Group of Members and Members of the First-signed Miho Kordis submitted to the legislative procedure a proposal to supplement the Health and Health Insurance Act 23. 6. 2020. The proposed amendment provides for the introduction of a new third paragraph of Article 45, which provides that the deficit created by the Health Insurance Office for the Covida-19 epidemic shall be covered by the provision of funds from the State budget. As you know, a number of measures have been taken in the context of the intervention legislation to mitigate the consequences of the epidemic, under which the State budget has assumed the obligation to finance certain content and has thus significantly burdened the health treasury. From the State budget, funds have been provided for hospital leave compensation since the first day of absence, and revenue shortfalls of the Health Insurance Office have also been replaced due to the exemption of health insurance contributions from compensation paid to workers on temporary leave for work, due to increased strength and for self-employed and farmers. The state budget has also provided funds for covidu-19 vaccination, for the transport of covido-19 patients, for covida-19 medicines, for rapid antigen testing and also for influenza vaccination. In 2020, 50 million Euros were provided from the State budget under the law on the implementation of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia to cover the costs associated with covida-19, and in 2021 these funds were even EUR 200 million. In doing so, let me explain that the Ministry of Monthly Level monitors the compliance of the institution's operations with the financial plan adopted and has an assessment of the financial implications of the covida-19 epidemic on the operations of the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia. The proposal does not address the long-term stability of healthcare financing, as all public treasury funds must, in accordance with the Fiscal Rule Act, ensure the medium-term balance of income and expenditure, nor provide for a time window in which the deficit of the institution would be covered by the State budget. Nor does the bill contain projections on the impact on fiscal sustainability of the budget, so it cannot be argued that such a solution would not pose a greater threat to the stability of public finances. In view of the increasing trend in health spending, we must note that the proposal for a law can have a negative impact on the overall fiscal framework. In order to achieve fiscal objectives during this period, expenditure on other items should also be reduced. Given the budget agreed in advance, this would mean cuts to other social security systems. In view of the above, the Government does not support the proposal to supplement the Health and Health Insurance Act. As so far, on the basis of the actual business data and the impact of the covida-19 epidemic on healthcare operations, the Government will continue its efforts to implement and adopt measures that will ensure the stable and smooth functioning of the institution and thus also of healthcare providers. Thank you.

In addition, the system is set up as it is, does not include any meaningful fuses that would represent one safety cushion in the event of crises. And this safety pillow needs our public health system; also for human stability, not only the fiscal nature of our country. As a result, this leads to an increase in supplementary health insurance premiums, a deterioration in the situation of the most vulnerable and a real increase in inequalities in Slovenia. Let me finish. The crisis has led to a significant reduction in the inflows to the health register due to an increase in unemployment, a slowdown in wages, an increase in the proportion of inactive debtors. And macroeconomic forecasts show that in the coming years the healthcare system will face financial pressures. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u42	Thank you for your word. Have a nice hello to you all again! The Health and Health Insurance Act of 1992 has been amended several times, but almost all of these 30 years have not seen any major changes and some other necessary changes. The amendment to the Health and Health Insurance Act proposed by the applicant relates to the current Article 45, which reads as follows: “The means of insurance obligation are provided by contributions paid to the Institute by insured persons, employers and others under this Act by certain undertakings. Contributions shall be paid from the basis laid down by this Act at proportionate rates." The proposed amendment to this Act, however, refers in explanation to two shortcomings in the provisions of the existing law, namely the absence of a budget protector, on the basis of which the institution’s deficit would be financed directly from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia during the crisis. As a second flaw, the proposaler states that the increased burden of financing healthcare is being passed back on to individuals, patients and patients as well. In view of the above, the applicant considers that the only reasonable solution is the introduction of the budgetary coverage of the increased expenditure of the Institute of Health Insurance from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia. Indeed, spending on health care is on the rise year after year, and in general it has increased during this co-vid crisis. However, due to an epidemic of collid crises in the budget, countries are provided with funding for measures taken to mitigate the consequences of the epidemic, which are later dedicated to the Health Insurance Office. At the same time, I would like to point out that we have adopted as many as 9 anti-Korona laws in this National Assembly on the elimination of the consequences of this crisis. The Malones also contain provisions on the basis of which funds are allocated from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia to remedy the consequences of the crisis to the Institute of Health Insurance, such as, for example, the coverage of the compensation for the absence of hospitals, the payment of health care providers due to the failure to carry out their activities and so on. We have already heard all these measures in the opening position of the Secretary of State. Likewise, the State's Framework Decision on the preparation of the budget of the sector for the period 2020-2022 was adopted in April this year in order to increase the public financial expenditure on health bills by 200 million. However, it can be reasonably expected that, in the light of the unscheduled or distorted programmes of healthcare providers in 2020 and also in 2021, the Institute of Health Insurance of Slovenia has fewer obligations to pay services to health care providers. At this address, the Institute should by no means have a negative result. In addition, I must add that in no way can I forget the repeated statement of the former Director of the Department of Health Insurance, Mr Fakin, who said several times at the meetings of the Health Committee of the mother: “The money in health is sufficient, only it should be properly distributed.” Secondly, the proposed amendment does not specify in which period, and also in what amount, the deficit would be covered by the Institute of Health Insurance from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia. Their proposal is therefore a blank exchange without a time limit and we cannot support this proposal in the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u86	Dear President! Dear Secretary of State! Proposal for a law supplementing the Act on the supplement to the pension for work and outstanding achievements in the field of sport. The Republic of Slovenia establishes a system of awarding top athletes after the end of their active careers, which have contributed significantly to the international reputation and visibility of the country by means of the Act on Pensions for Work and Exceptional Achievements in Sport. Top athletes, winners of Olympic medals, from world championships are thus entitled to an allowance for old age and invalid pension. The pension consists of a pension as a sum of payments to the pension fund and an allowance. At the time of the adoption of the law in 2017, we particularly welcomed the inclusion of disabled athletes among pensioners in this law. We have stressed that their achievements also make an important contribution to the development of sport, as their achievements in the sport field are enriched by inspiring stories that testify to the fact that, despite obstacles, they can also achieve top-notch results. That is why we have tabled a proposal for a new Law on the Appendix to the Pension for Work and Exceptional Achievements in Sport, which eliminates discrimination and unequal treatment of top athletes with disabilities. With the amendment of the law, we propose that the round of beneficiaries of the pension allowance provided for in Article 3, paragraph 1, of the Law extend to top athletes disabled, winners of medals from world championships in the Paralympic collective and individual sports disciplines and winners of medals from the World Deaf Championships. The world championships in the system of sport for disabled persons and non-disabled persons, regardless of the system of organisation of sports industries and associations, are comparable or equivalent to cities. In this case, in his assessment of the discriminatory nature of the law, the Advocate for the principle of equality assessed discrimination against the top athletes of disabled persons. Article 2 of the Anti-Discrimination Act explicitly provides protection against discrimination on the basis of personal circumstances in areas related to social protection, including social security and social benefits. However, the Supreme Court ruled on this issue of unequal treatment of individuals, since the review of the case against the judgement of the Administrative Court ruled that sports achievements of disabled athletes from the World Paralympic Discipline Championships must also be taken into account in the decision to add to the pension for disabled top athletes. With the adoption of the proposed novel, we will clearly write down the legal provision that disabled athletes who have received a medal from the World Paralympic Discipline Championships from the World Deaf Championships will also be entitled to the pension allowance, thus eliminating discrimination and ensuring equal sport treatment of disabled athletes and athletes. It is likely that soon the achievements of the Olympic and Paralympic Games will be able to watch on TV and cheer for our athletes, who are the best promoter of Slovenia in the world in these difficult times. And I hope that by confirming this law you will prove it. Thank you very much.

As soon as their career ends, most of them face existential problems, especially when they are no longer in the soybeans of the media. Then they are no longer in the public eye, and this one likes to forget their success. Not to mention that in the fall of their lives, the few often find that their sports organisations for which they have been engaged for many years have not properly paid contributions to pension insurance or have been minimal, and therefore today they have the lowest pension base, which means that their benefits are bottling. Above all, they are forgotten by politics and are ultimately left alone because many do not finish schooling, are without education and so on. After their sports retirement, the country does not have a solution in the form of a job, a pension or an extraordinary pension, even though they were ambassadors to Slovenia during their sports careers around the world. At that time, the law allowed top athletes, who, with their work and outstanding achievements in the field of sport, made a lasting contribution to the development of society, reputation and visibility of the country, to receive an allowance for pensions if they received an Olympic or Paralympic medal, a medal with the Olympic Games of the Deaf, a medal with the Chess Olympics or a medal from world championships in the Olympic collective sports industries or Olympic disciplines, individual sports sectors in the competition. Among the beneficiaries of the supplement were the recipients of the Bloudk Award for Lifetime Work or the top international sports achievements. The 2017 Regulation was based on a system and organisation of sport at international level, setting out competitions which have the greatest international competition and are duly ranked in order to be able to be properly evaluated. The shift in understanding the meaning of the pension allowance and the evaluation of outstanding achievements in the field of sport was achieved in February this year for the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia. This says that exceptional sports achievements are determined on the basis of two criteria – on the basis of the rank of the competition and on the basis of the ranking of the competition. As different Olympics and world championships are included in the competitions, the legal comparability of sports results achieved in various sports competitions and in different disciplines or sectors is established in a way that all of these achievements correspond to the common denominator; and this is the exceptionality of achievement. All the above results should therefore be treated in the same way. The purpose of the law is therefore to reward exceptional achievements, and equality before the law also requires the same application of the criterion at the level of competition in a way that, if non-disabled athletes take into account the world championship in certain sectors or disciplines, the same should apply to disabled athletes. This is why we are putting an end to the discriminatory regime today. We also include top athletes, winners of medals from world championships in the Paralympic collective and individual sports sectors or disciplines. With a supplement on the home committee, we extended the range of beneficiaries to medal winners from the World Deaf Championships. On the basis of all that has been said, we are pleased to be able to make a decision on the bill and, of course, we are very pleased to support it unanimously. In advance, good luck to all those who engage in sports and thank all athletes for their results!

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u126	Thank you for your word, chairman. Nice greetings to all present, dear Members, colleagues! The proposed amendments and additions to the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, which are before us today, are the result of long-term preparations and coordination between the parliamentary groups of the National Assembly. The proposal pursues two objectives. The first objective is to amend Rule 118 of the Rules of Procedure, which allows the proposer to have his proposal at his disposal throughout the legislative procedure. It can also be withdrawn just before the hearing, which it has not been able to do so far and the proposal for a law has nevertheless been dealt with in the National Assembly, despite the proposal's reservations. The aim is to ensure that the National Assembly is not burdened by proposals for laws in which the proposer himself considers that the reasons for their withdrawal are. The second objective is to harmonise the regime of parliamentary immunity with other laws. The Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly has found that there is a certain discrepancy between the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the Law on Members and the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly in the regulation of the procedural immunity of Members of the National Assembly. Immunity is a Member's privilege to exempt it from the application of individual legal rules applicable to other citizens, but the primary purpose of parliamentary immunity is not to protect a Member as an individual, but to protect the uninterrupted part of the National Assembly. The proposed amendments and additions, in addition to harmonisation, also provide a clearer distinction between the prior recognition of immunity based on the request of the competent national authority when the conditions for determining the detention of a Member or the initiation of a criminal procedure are laid down and the subsequent recognition of immunity based on the notification of the competent national authority. The essential ingredients to be included in the request or notification shall also be specified in more detail. The duties of the President of the National Assembly with regard to the transmission of the request and the notification to the Management Election Commission shall also be clearly defined. However, a key novelty is that the National Assembly has notified the initiation of a criminal procedure against a Member who does not refer to immunity is dealt with optionally, i.e. when there are reasonable grounds for deciding whether immunity is necessary for the functioning of the National Assembly. However, the procedure shall be carried out only if the President of the National Assembly so proposes, a delegation whose member is a member of the Member to whom the immunity relates, or a Member, if he is not a member of any parliamentary group. The applicant must state the reasons for the decision on subsequent immunity. If none of the eligible applicants proposes a decision on immunity, the National Assembly shall not decide on immunity. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u130	Thank you again for the word, chairman. Have a nice hello again! The current regulation restricts the law's proposers from withdrawing or replacing the law with a new one. According to the new proposal, it is possible for the applicants to have the legislative proposal at their disposal all the time, either in a way to withdraw it fully or to replace it with another. The proposed solution will have an important impact on the legislative process in order not to burden itself needlessly. The second set of amendments concerns the amended regime of the procedure relating to parliamentary immunity, in particular due to the need to comply with other acts. According to the new Rules of Procedure, the procedure for prior and subsequent decisions on immunity will also be more specifically regulated. The prior recognition of immunity will be based on the request of the competent national authority, which will have to have all the prescribed ingredients in order to be subject to consideration. Subsequently recognised immunity will be based on a notification from the competent authority. It should also be noted that the amended provisions of the Rules of Procedure will also apply to decisions on the immunity of the judge, the Ombudsman and his deputy. Emissary immunity has meaning and meaning. Its aim is to ensure the smooth work of Parliament as the supreme representative body in the democratic system, but not at all and not at all costs. Immunity should only be granted in cases where the prosecution of a Member significantly impedes the normal work of Parliament, provided that neither the gravity nor the type of criminal offence are so severe and rejected that the recognition of immunity demoralises the legal order. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u134	We will, of course, support the proposal for amendments and the addition of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly in the Group of Non-Aligned Members. As you know, the proposed amendments and additions are the result of the coordination between the leadership of the National Assembly, the professional services, the legislative and legal services and the parliamentary groups, and the members and members of the Rules of Procedure Commission, with these coordinations taking almost three years. The first set of main solutions is an amendment to Rule 118 of the Rules of Procedure, which will allow the bill's proposer to withdraw or replace it with a new proposal just prior to the treatment on the home labour body, which he has not been able to do until now. However, the prerequisite for such a decision will, of course, be the assessment that there are sufficient reasons for this, once it is generally familiar with the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the opinion of the Government and of course the amendments tabled. In the parliamentary group, we welcome the proposed solution; the proposer may already consider the opinions and views of the proposed proposal by this point of the legislative process and on the basis of these estimates that he would not be supported by it, or that appropriate amendments or harmonisation of the proposal could not be achieved by means of amendments. The aim of this is to ease the legislative process with which we agree by unrelated Members. The second set of amendments concerns the regulation of procedural parliamentary immunity. Immunity is the privilege of Members to be exempt from the application of law enforcement rules that apply to all individuals suspected of serving a crime. In doing so, it should be noted that this exemption is not unconditional, but only applied in cases where the aim is to protect the uninterrupted part of the National Assembly. It should be noted that in this case only procedural changes are concerned. The Rules of Procedure do not separate procedures when it comes to deciding on immunity on the basis of a Member's request and when it comes to deciding on immunity only on the basis of a notification from the competent authority in the criminal procedure in which the Member was brought. The National Assembly shall also decide on immunity if the Member does not refer to it at all, on the basis of a notification by the competent authority that the Member is in detention or criminal proceedings for a criminal offence with a penalty of more than five years. However, the proposed amendment to the Rules of Procedure would separate the immunity procedure when the Member refers to it and the procedure where the Member does not invoke that immunity. If no one proposes a decision on immunity, the National Assembly will not decide on it. Otherwise, a qualified proposer will also have to justify why the decision on immunity is necessary for the smooth functioning of the National Assembly. In this regard, we would also like to draw attention to our contribution to the proposed solutions. The initial proposal of the services provided that a proposal for a subsequent decision on immunity could be submitted by a parliamentary group whose member is a Member to which the notification relates or by a Member to which the notification relates if it is not a member of any member group. Now, however, the possibility of proposing this procedure in the proposal has also expanded to the President of the National Assembly. The last proposal was based on the fact that the President of the National Assembly was first called to take care of the smooth functioning of the National Assembly. In addition, it must take care of the integrity of the National Assembly and the reputation of the parliamentary function. On the basis of the above arguments, we will support these changes in the parliamentary group. However, we regret that the National Assembly has not been able to reach agreement that the Institute of Parliamentary Immunity should be more adequately regulated at the constitutional level, as some experts have been alerting for many years. We believe that we should consider reshaping this institution, which, of course, is not possible with the changes to the Rules of Procedure, but simply with the amendment of the Constitution.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u135	Thank you very much. It is Dušan Šiško who will present the position of the Slovenian National Party's Parliamentary Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u136	Your Honor! One of the two main objectives of this proposal for a revision of the Rules of Procedure is that the draft law can be withdrawn by the beginning of the consideration of the bill and not only by the conclusion of the meeting at which the law will be examined. This gives the applicant the opportunity to be able to dispose of the draft law throughout the proceedings. The applicant shall be given more autonomy, since if he considers that there is a reason for the withdrawal of the law, he shall do so, even if it is just before the hearing. The second amendment concerns changes in the procedural immunity of Members. It would make sense to make a change that would include substantive parliamentary immunity from civil liability, as it ensures free expression of opinions, proposals and views without fear of any responsibility. A large number of countries from Europe are aware of such arrangements. The constitutional regime, which limits parliamentary immunity only to the exception of criminal liability and does not exclude the Member's civil responsibility for the said words in Parliament, raises doubts about parliamentary democracy. The latter must ensure the full independence of the legislative branch of authority from the executive branch of the judiciary. Members' immunity from civil liability would allow them to avoid certain political farcees because Members have a wide range of freedom of speech. It would provide a guardian to maintain a certain level of cultural political dialogue in Parliament. Nevertheless, the participation of Members in any procedure may significantly impede the functioning of Parliament, leading to the Member being unable to carry out his duties in Parliament. Obstacles to operation can be seen mainly in this reference, where any voice is important and the problem of operation can also arise due to the absence of patients on a critical day. Parliament's decisions are so important for the country that it is essential for their adoption to ensure the optimal conditions for Members' opinions to be expressed freely and constructively.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u137	Thank you very much. Boris Doblekar will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u138	Thank you, Chairman! This amendment and the addition of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly are two amendments and one addition. Immunity generally means the right or privilege of an individual to be exempt from the application of individual legal provisions applicable to other persons. The fundamental purpose of parliamentary immunity is to enable Parliament, as the highest representative body in the country, to work independently and smoothly and to ensure the conditions for the full and free functioning of its members in the exercise of the function in which they were elected. All modern constitutions therefore generally recognise parliamentary immunity as a guarantee of the Member's legal security and independence in the performance of his duties. The main objective of the proposed amendments and the addition of the Rules of Procedure is therefore to make a more specific distinction between the procedure for the so-called preliminary decision on immunity referred to in Article 83(2) of the Constitution and the procedure for the so-called subsequent decision on immunity referred to in Article 83(3) of the Constitution, since there are two different situations which require a different and more detailed procedure for the decision-making of the National Assembly on immunity. The proposed amendments and additions to the Rules of Procedure introduce clearer, more transparent and appropriate legal arrangements for both procedures for deciding on Member's immunity, in which the National Assembly examines the request of the competent national authority to allow detention against a Member or for the authorisation of the National Assembly to initiate a criminal procedure against a Member and to notify the competent national authority of the arrangements for detention against a Member or the notice of initiation of a criminal procedure against a Member. The proposal introduces a new regulation of the procedure for examining the notification by the competent national authority of the course of criminal proceedings against a Member and implements the constitutional provision referred to in Article 83(3) more appropriately than the applicable rules of procedure. The Supplement to the Rules of Procedure is also proposed in the work relating to the immunity of the judge and the Ombudsman or his deputy, since the judicial immunity of the judge is subject to the order of detention and the conduct of the criminal procedure; and only the detention of the Ombudsman or his deputy. The proposed amendments concerning the immunity of a Member which distinguish between the procedure relating to the request and the procedure concerning the notification of a competent national authority require a supplement setting out clearly which procedure relating to the immunity of a Member is to be taken into account in cases of decisions on the immunity of a judge, the Ombudsman and the Deputy Ombudsman. The proposed amendment to the first paragraph of Article 118 means that the applicant may withdraw the proposal from the legislative procedure or replace it by the beginning of the first reading of the draft law at the meeting of the National Assembly or by the beginning of the second reading of the draft law at the meeting of the home working body, it shall be said that he will, as a rule, be familiar with the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the opinion of the Government, the amendments tabled and so on. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we will support the proposed amendments to the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, as we have also actively participated in these amendments. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u140	Thank you very much, Vice President, for your word. Dear colleagues, dear colleagues! The proposal to amend and supplement the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, which we are considering today, brings two important substantive solutions. The first relates to amendments to the Rules of Procedure concerning the immunity of Members. The Rules of Procedure will provide for two separate procedures, the so-called preliminary or subsequent decision-making procedure of the National Assembly on Member's immunity. Depending on whether or not the request of the competent authority for the authorisation of detention or the authorisation of the initiation of a criminal procedure will be made, or whether it will be a notification to the competent authority of the detention or initiation of a criminal procedure. Procedures relating to the immunity of Members will be streamlined in such a way as to ensure that the Mandate-Electorial Commission and the National Assembly decide on such matters on an up-to-date basis, but only in cases where this will be urgently necessary and meaningful under the Constitution and the Rules of Procedure; not in all cases, as has been the case so far. The new regulation of the Member's immunity procedure will also apply mutatis mutandis in certain business arrangements in any decision-making procedure on the immunity of the judge and the Ombudsman or his deputy. However, the second amendment of the Rules of Procedure allows the bill-holder to withdraw or replace it by a new proposal until the first reading of the legislative proposal at the meeting of the National Assembly or the second reading of the legal proposal at the meeting of the home working body, of course, on condition that it has not previously been discussed in a general discussion at the meeting of the National Assembly. The purpose of this amendment is, in particular, to allow the drafter of the law to withdraw or replace the text even after a meeting of the National Assembly or the meeting of the home working body has already been convened, at which the proposal for a law is to be considered, and subsequently, significant substantive comments have been made to it and cannot be taken into account or taken into account in sufficient consideration by the possible additions to the article of the law. Therefore, the drafter will have the opportunity to withdraw the proposal from the legislative procedure or replace it with a new proposal which will, of course, contain appropriate solutions or answers to previously expressed comments. We consider the proposed amendments and additions to the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly in the Group of Social Democrats to be useful and necessary and will therefore give them unanimous support. It is, however, a fact that in the near future we are still facing some urgent changes to the Rules of Procedure, not least as regards the procedure for the adoption of an authentic interpretation of the law, which the Constitutional Court has recently decided is not in accordance with the principle of the division of power and the principle of the independence of judges, so it is also contrary to the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. Perhaps it is the subsequent decision of the National Assembly on immunity after the start of the persecution. For worse crimes which are punishable by a prison sentence of more than 5 years and for all cases where the Member does not invoke immunity. Due to different flows in both forms of procedural immunity, the procedure for both forms of procedure should also be set up differently and separately in the Rules of Procedure. The novelty is mainly a new procedure for the subsequent decision of the National Assembly on immunity. Presentation of recast procedures, first case, preliminary ruling of the National Assembly on immunity. The competent State authority shall send an application for the authorisation of detention or for the authorisation of the commencement of criminal proceedings to the President of the National Assembly. The President shall transmit this to the Mandate-Matrimonial Commission. The mandate-holder committee may propose to the National Assembly whether or not to grant immunity. As a rule, the recognition of immunity may be considered necessary for the exercise of a Member's function in the event that the competent national authority intends to bring a Member into custody or when it has already detained him, but not in the event that it intends to initiate a criminal procedure against him or when he has already begun. The same Rules of Procedure already apply. And the final decision is taken by the National Assembly. And another case, the subsequent immunity decision. The competent State authority shall send to the President of the National Assembly a notification of the detention or initiation of a criminal procedure. The President shall transmit this to the Mandate-Matrimonial Commission. The President of the National Assembly shall inform the parliamentary group whose member is the Member to which the notification relates. The President of the National Assembly or the parliamentary group of which the Member is a member may request that this notice be examined by the National Assembly. The MVK may propose to the National Assembly that immunity be granted subsequently or not. As a rule, recognition of immunity may be deemed necessary for the exercise of a Member's function in the event that the competent national authority intends to arrest or has already detained a Member, but not in the event that it intends to initiate a criminal procedure against him or when he has already begun. The same Rules of Procedure already apply. The final decision shall be taken by the National Assembly. If the National Assembly subsequently recognises the immunity of a Member, the criminal procedure shall no longer be brought against him or a detained Member shall immediately be released. And a temporary decision in case of immediate effect. In any event, the mandate-holder commission will be able to decide immediately in any event on the recognition of immunity from detention at the time of the request for detention. This was possible only after the regulation had been drawn up so far in the event that the Member had already been detained. The decision, as it has been so far, is effective immediately, and the National Assembly can change it later. In view of the different nature of both forms of procedural immunity, it is appropriate to separate the procedure for each of them. This will lead to a clearer and more predictable procedure in each case. The amendment extending the deadline within which the withdrawal of the bill is possible reduces the important administrative burden within the legislative procedure and consequently bureaucraticly facilitates the work of the National Assembly. We will therefore support the proposal.

The organization TIGR – named after the beginnings of Trieste, Istria, Gorica, Rijeka – was formed in September 1927 to resist Italian fascist oppression. Among other things, tigers organized a wide-ranging intelligence network in Primorska prior to the war, and collected information was sent to the intelligence centre of the Drava Division in Ljubljana and later to the British Intelligence Service. Even so, and the fact that they were involved in the war in the OF, the communist Februarys had already looked at them with considerable mistrust, which had only increased since the war. Tigers were pushed to the side track and removed from the collective memory, making it necessary to wait until 1991 for the first marking of the Battle of Mali and the official ceremony until 2005. The amendment of the law proposed a new State holiday, May 13 – the day of the first conflict with the invader on Slovenian soil, which would not be a day off. In Slovenia we already have several national holidays, where we are reminded of various historical events that were more or less important in our past. The first armed conflict with the occupier is one of the important events in the history of our people, so we have supported the amendment of the law on the parental committee, but unfortunately the proposal has not received sufficient support. Unfortunately, such a new State holiday will not be celebrated and these events will be commemorated. It is certainly right not to forget our history and learn something from it, not to repeat the same mistakes that have already been made, but to have a history as a teacher of life. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u160	Thank you for your word, chairman. Dear Members and Members! The proposal before you today was prepared due to the obligation of the Republic of Slovenia to transpose Directive 2018/957/EU into the national legal order. The key solutions transmitted by the proposal are the principle of applying the same rules on payment for the work performed by posted workers, as is the case for local workers in accordance with legislation and universal collective agreements at the level of the country of deployment, the time limit on long-term posting periods and the obligation to ensure compliance with the principle of equal treatment where cross-border services are provided by posted workers employed by the employer for the provision of work. In addition to the transposition of that Directive, the proposal contains some corrections resulting from a preliminary assessment by the European Commission of the Slovenian national measures for the transposition of Directive 2014/67 on the posting of workers in the framework of the performance. The proposal to amend the law before you today thus represents only the transposition of Directive 2018/957/EU and thus the fulfilment of the obligations that Slovenia, as a Member State, has to the European Commission. In addition to the implementation of the Directive into the national legal order, we have also removed disproportionate measures that we have been alerted to by the European Commission. Thus, the legislative material does not contain substantive changes beyond the provisions of the Directive and the previous assessment by the European Commission. As part of the drafting of the amendments to the law, we have received calls for further areas that go beyond simply the transposition of the directive into the national legal order. The law itself will therefore continue to be the subject of further negotiations and consideration within the negotiating group established for this purpose. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u165	Thank you very much. Mr Jani Prednik will present the position of the SD Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u166	Good afternoon, everyone! With the proposed amendment to the Cross-border Service Implementation Act, the Republic of Slovenia transposes into its national legislation amendments to European rules relating to workers who are assigned to work in another Member State in order to temporarily perform a specific service there. At European Union level, the rules on posted workers have changed in order to ensure an appropriate balance between workers' rights and the freedom to provide services in another Member State of the European Union. These amendments also seek to counter abuse, such as social dumping, which is a practice in which different rules apply to different categories of workers in salaries and in paying social contributions, resulting in unfair competition at the expense of workers' rights. The general objective of the new rules is thus to ensure equal pay for the work carried out by posted workers, as is the case for local workers, in accordance with the legislation and general collective agreements of the Member State to which the worker has been posted; it is required to respect the equal treatment of all workers even when an agency worker is posted to another Member State. In addition, the amendment of the law also amends those parts of the legislation which the European Commission, in its preliminary assessment of the transposition of the Directive, has assessed as disproportionate to the requirements of the Directive. The European Commission has drawn Slovenia's attention to the excessive administrative burden of foreign service providers in Slovenia on the application of the start-up of services, in particular in relation to the requested information on the service provider's contact person and the information on the service provider's service provider, as the European Commission estimates that they are unnecessary in the service application process. It also amends the rules relating to the requirements for the submission of working legal documentation translated into Slovenian, as it is already possible for the Labour Inspectorate to have access to translations of foreign documentation through the Internal Market Information System. We are aware that the Republic of Slovenia is committed to the transposition of European rules into national legislation in the field of cross-border service delivery, so we will not oppose the proposal of the law.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u169	Thank you very much. The position of the SMC Group will be presented by Mrs Mojca Žnidarič. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u170	Thank you for your word, Vice President. Nice greetings to all present! The main objective of the proposed amendment to the Cross-border Service Implementation Act is to comply with European legislation, which is to amend the Posting of Workers Directive in the framework of the provision of services. The Directive contains urgent changes relating to the establishment of an appropriate balance between the need to promote freedom to provide services and to ensure a level playing field and the need to protect the rights of posted workers to work in another Member State of the European Union. The implementation of that Directive provides for the principle of the application of the same payment rules for posted workers as is the case for local workers in accordance with the legislation and generally applicable collective agreements at the level of the country of dispatch. It also regulates relations between Slovenia and foreign service providers who work with us for the Slovenian employer. However, Slovenian companies in other European countries, with their posted workers under the Directive, will also have to comply with the labour rules and standards in those same countries. In view of the unsatisfactory and often inadequate application of common rules in the Member States, the proposal itself also followed the necessary amendments we are considering today, following the adoption and transposition of the implementing and basic Directives. The basic guide of the changes is the abolition of social or wage dumping, which is made possible at European Union level by different national arrangements in individual Member States. The amendment in question introduces additional conditions on the basis of which the user may provide services to workers who have been sent to him on the basis of an agreement with the employer for the provision of work. These are provisions resulting from respect for the principle of equal treatment when cross-border services are provided by posted workers employed by the employer for the provision of work. There should be no dilemma as to who the posted workers are. It is the workers who are employed by the employer to provide work. They are deemed to have been directed to the place of work by their employer and not by the company user who actually provides the service for the contracting authority in the Republic of Slovenia. The amendment also regulates the situation of posted workers in the Republic of Slovenia who are posted to temporary employment by a foreign employer under a contract of employment concluded under foreign law. Thus, the Institute of Payment for Work and not just the concept of minimum hourly items shall apply to posted workers. The scope of labour legislation is also extended to workers who are referred to users by employers to provide work in the context of cross-border service delivery. Cases shall also be regulated where the actual duration of the referral exceeds 12 months and 18 months in the event of an extension in accordance with the rules governing the cross-border provision of services. In such cases, the employer will have to grant the worker rights under the regulations of the Republic of Slovenia governing the employment relationship and under the provisions of the collective agreement at the level of activity, if this is more favourable for the worker. The posting of an employee shall normally be limited to 12 months but may be extended to 18 months only on the basis of a reasoned request. It is a matter of time-limiting long-term posting periods, with the periods of posting, when it comes to the posting of workers for the provision of similar services at the same workplace, being summed up. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we believe that the solutions are appropriate, and we will support the proposal of the law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u172	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Hello again. Your Honor! One of the legal areas in which we all meet in different roles is labour law. The way the state regulates it is very important because it regulates conflicting interests. On the one hand, the employer's interests, on the other hand, the worker's interest. Since the first is superior to the second, the protection of the rights of the worker has been shaped as a fundamental principle of labour law, in particular with a view to balancing the power between the employer and the worker. Over the years, various international organisations have emerged and resolutions and standards have been adopted setting the minimum level of workers' rights to be taken into account by countries in the drafting of their national legislation. And if, at this level, working relationships in Europe are largely fairly equitable, the fundamental principles of uniformity are increasingly difficult to achieve at this level of cross-border service flows. One of the institutes set up at EU level is the legal institute for the posting of workers designed to facilitate the movement of services on the one hand and to ensure the protection of workers' rights on the other. This is a compromise between the provisions on free movement of services and social security and between EU-level regulation and national arrangements of individual countries. The draft law before us thus introduces the transposition of the EU Directive, harmonising the effects of different national legislation on working conditions for the performance of posted workers. Its transposition into national law provides, according to the applicant, the principle of applying the same rules regarding payment for the work performed by posted workers, as is the case for local workers in accordance with the laws and general collective agreements at the level of the country of destination. The relationship between the Republic of Slovenia and foreign service providers in the Republic of Slovenia is therefore being regulated, while Slovenian companies in other European countries will also have to comply with the labour rules and standards in those countries. The proposal also provides for mandatory compliance with the principle of equal treatment in cases where cross-border services are provided by so-called agency workers, with the current minimum standards being replaced by standards in accordance with the legislation and collective agreements in force at the level of the Member State in which the services are provided. The new arrangement also foresees that posted workers are employed all the time with the employer and not with the company that provides the service for the contracting authority in the Republic of Slovenia. We have no major concerns in the SAB Group on the transposition of the directive, so we will support the bill. In doing so, we draw attention to open questions. The Economic and Social Council negotiating group failed to agree on a provision that would take into account the salaries received abroad as a basis for the payment of contributions to pension and disability insurance. Currently it is considered to be the basis of the salary that would be paid for the same work in Slovenia, which is often significantly lower than the actual payment received. This dilemma should not hang in the air. Thank you very much.

It lays down a period of five years in which it is mandatory for holders of existing arms instruments to apply for the classification of weapons in the relevant categories as required by the Directive and this Act and provides for the legalisation of weapons. This is the first defence proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the law on organisation and police work within the framework of the ordinary procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u238	Dear Presidents, honourable Members, allow me to briefly present the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the organisation and work of the police, which, in our view, has been shown to be the biggest remedy for the shortcomings which have been demonstrated in practice in the implementation of the applicable law so far. The aim of this law is to ensure a more flexible and better organisation of the police, so that it can respond more quickly to all security challenges and that the implementation of the requirements of the professional agreement, that is, from the part where we agreed with the unions, how to proceed, is also de facto implemented. I will present the most important proposed amendments and amendments to the law very quickly and essential solutions are the following. When assessing the procedure under the Police Guidance and Control Rules, there is a dilemma as to whether it is a form of control and what powers supervisors can use. The proposal defines the so-called reporting requirement as part of the supervisory activity, lays down the powers of supervisors and the legal basis for obtaining data from the event record and the rights of public servants carrying out surveillance of police work in the form of a permanent allowance. Because of the different views on the Minister's competence to guide the police in the pre-trial proceedings, the situation in which the Prosecutor was informed of the crime is more clearly regulated, but he did not take the lead. In this case, police officers act independently within the framework of legal powers and are subject to supervision by their superiors. In view of the amendment to the Regulation on the cooperation of the State Prosecutor's Office, the police and other competent national authorities and institutions in the detection and prosecution of offenders and the functioning of specialised and joint investigation teams, which is issued under Article 160a, paragraph 5, of the Criminal Procedure Act, and further regulates all activities also with regard to the taking-over of police work in the pre-trial procedure, the amendment proposes the deletion of various guidelines or different arrangements relating to these powers. With regard to the National Investigation Office, I would like to point out that this is an internal organisational unit of the Directorate of Criminal Police. The law makes it clear that the Office is autonomous in its investigation of crimes and in its way of determining which investigations into suspected crimes will take place. Not like I said yesterday that it can't be self-serving. The draft law also deletes the provisions on the appointment and discharge of the Director-General of the National Investigation Office and the procedure is the same as for all other public servants. The internal security records maintained by the police in the administration for the purpose of carrying out police duties and ensuring internal security shall be determined and shall be supplemented by provisions on the security verification of tenderers, customers and persons for the performance of police work or for the performance of works at the premises of the police in accordance with a contract signed by them in accordance with the rules governed by public procurement laws. It shall also specify a limitation for a policeman who shall not be a member of political parties during the duration of the relationship and shall not act as a professional mayor or a professional sub-discipliner or a municipal councillor. A procedure shall be laid down for obtaining positions in the posts of Head of Organizational Units at the first level of the General Police Administration, the Director of Police Administration and the Chief of Police, which shall no longer be the Chief of Staff; it shall be named after a new one, by temporary redeployment for a period of five years and by reasons for termination. In accordance with the professional agreement, it is stipulated that the employees of the police and public servants of the Ministry who carry out support tasks for the police may be paid up to 20% of the basic salary due to increased labour and special burdens. It is specified that police officers are not entitled to strike at the time of their operation under Article 107, paragraph 2, of the Law on the Organisation and Work of the Police in the event of police operations in the event of natural and other accidents, in crisis, in war or emergency. The amendments provide for better organisation of the police in the use of the auxiliary police, so that it is clearly determined who cannot participate in the auxiliary police. In compliance with the conditions, it is possible to conclude an auxiliary police contract even after the age of 60, the payment of the auxiliary police officer and the reimbursement of the costs and the conditions for termination or termination of the contract with the candidate. Your Honor! I believe that the solutions presented in the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the organisation and work of the police are appropriate and I propose that the bill be supported.
