ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u249	Thank you very much. The position of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre will be presented by mag. Dušan Verbich. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u250	Thank you for your word, Vice President of the National Assembly. Nice greetings from the Minister with a co-worker! Dear Members and Members, you too have a good greeting! Today we have a first reading of the draft law on amendments and additions to the Law on Organising and Working in the Police, which was tabled by the Government in the parliamentary procedure. The aim of the bill is to remedy some of the shortcomings that have been demonstrated in the implementation of the law in practice. The main guide in the preparation of the changes is the more effective functioning of the police than the whole. Let me give you a few areas that are undergoing the necessary changes or additions. The provisions on the direction and control of police work are being amended or supplemented. As a result, the article defining the so-called reporting requirement and the new article setting out the rights of public servants exercising control over police work shall also be supplemented. Under the first paragraph of Article 3 of the Regulation, police officers shall inform the public prosecutors of all cases established by this Regulation where there are grounds for suspicion that a crime has been committed. The second paragraph of Article 5 of the Regulation provides that the competent State prosecutor may, upon receipt of the notification immediately and within five days at the latest, submit to the police written instructions and proposals for action in the forecasting procedure in accordance with Article 12 of this Regulation. If he fails to submit instructions and proposals at that time, he shall be deemed not to direct the forecasting procedure but may be subsequently included in the cases in question. For the purposes of this Regulation, the guidance of the forecasting procedure shall be set out in Articles 10 to 18 thereof. The first paragraph of Article 10 of the Regulation provides that a police officer dealing with a particular case must inform of the activities carried out and the results of the State Prosecutor when directing the forecasting procedure. If the Public Prosecutor does not take the lead, the police shall act independently within the framework of the legal powers they have to detect perpetrators and criminal offences and may apply all legal measures in this context. In order to avoid the exchange of information and guidance that is present in the existing dictatorship of paragraph 8 of this Article, and to misinterpret and explain that the competent State prosecutor conducts targeted police work in pre-arranged procedures in all cases where he has been informed of the crime, the proposed formulation is more appropriate and also allows it to determine whether the competent State Prosecutor has really guided or directs the police's work in the pre-arrangement procedure for a particular offence. The proposed amendment does not change the Minister's mandate, as the Law on Police Organisation and Work and the Police Guidance and Control Regulation already regulate the systematic and planned provision of mandatory instructions and guidance for police work in terms of assessing the legality, protection of human rights and the actual situation in a particular area of police activity. In accordance with criminal procedural law, in our forecasting system, the sole prosecutor is the one who can direct the police in the exercise of police powers in the forecasting process and the proposed amendment does not change this in any way. Only the situation in which the state prosecutor was informed of the crime is resolved, but has not taken the lead, and therefore the Minister's direction in relation to the provision in force. In these cases, the police are not subject to any supervision, which may be inadequate and the proposed amendment also eliminates them. The amendment also makes it clearer to determine the autonomy of the National Investigation Office and the manner in which investigations into suspected crimes will be undertaken by the Office. It is also established that the National Investigation Office, in carrying out its tasks, is autonomous and, more specifically, provides that this Office is autonomous in the investigation of criminal offences. It is an internal organisational unit of the Directorate of Criminal Police, which means that its autonomy is not absolute, but that the unit and the Director of the Office are subordinated to the Director of the Directorate of Criminal Police. The National Investigation Office shall be autonomous in the investigation of offences referred to in Article 21, paragraph 1, of the law. In addition, the proposal for a novelty governs the security verification of tenderers, customers and persons for the performance of police duties or for the performance of works at police premises in accordance with a contract concluded under public procurement or bonding rules. All tenderers and persons referred to in the new article have already been screened by the police in accordance with the Article of the Law on Organising and Working in the Police. The police will also re-examine a client who is not a provider under public procurement rules and intends to conclude a contract for the execution of works with the police, in order to ensure the security of data, staff, premises, facilities or the environment of the police, where this security cannot be ensured through the measures set out in the facility protection plan, the house order or other security measures. These are examples in which the Ministry of the Interior or the police conclude with a party, for example, an author's translation contract, and we can specifically say interpretation. So far, the police have not been able to verify the security of the parties who have not entered into a contract under public procurement rules. These parties pose a risk to the security of data, staff, premises, facilities or the environment of the police. With a preventive measure, it is said by a security check, the police will carry out the identified risks. The novel also sets a limit for a policeman who must not be a member of a political party during the working relationship and must not serve as a professional mayor, a professional mayor or a municipal councilor. The proposed amendment establishes the principle of political neutrality for police officers who are not allowed to act politically. At the conclusion of the working relationship, a police officer must make a written declaration that he is not a member of a political party and that membership in a political party is also a reason for the withdrawal of the right to exercise police powers. One of the key solutions is that in the case of a former police officer's re-employment, it is possible to appoint to the same level of title and classification as the same salary class in which the former police officer was appointed or classified prior to the termination of the police employment relationship. In the police itself, there is a great interest in the re-employment of former police officers who, at the time of their employment with education, training and work experience, have acquired and adopted knowledge specific to the police profession and are thus qualified to carry out police work on their own immediately. However, since police officers were generally classified in higher salary classes prior to the end of their employment, and the differences are mostly more than five wage classes, this has a significant negative impact on their material financial situation, which is why, despite their mutual interest, they do not choose to re-employment in the police. With the option of re-employment in the same title and salary class, it would further stimulate potential former police officers with already adopted very needed police knowledge to re-employment in the police, and this would be highly desirable. The novel also provides for better police organisation in the use of auxiliary police and provides, in accordance with the strike agreement concluded, that police officers and public ministry officials carrying out police support tasks may be paid up to 20% of the basic salary due to increased workload and special burdens. This is a solution that is comparable by content to an increase in wages due to an increase in labour and the specific burdens imposed on members of the Slovenian Armed Forces in the Service Act in the Slovenian Army. At the end. The novel also states that a short-term service weapon, which he has had in his personal charge, or another short-term short-term firearms that is older than 12 years of age may be awarded to a policeman after his retirement if a police officer makes a written wish to possess this weapon and makes a written declaration that he will apply for the issuance of an arms document within three months of his retirement. A certificate shall be issued on the intended distribution of firearms. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we believe that the solutions are good, so we will vote in favour of this change. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u254	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The police have their mission, it's to help people, to take care of their security and property security. In the last 15 months, the covida-19 epidemic has marked police work. A number of government measures have affected the structures, systems and processes of the police, which, in addition to regular tasks, have also received new responsibilities to monitor the implementation of numerous and rapidly changing measures to combat the epidemic. The Police Organisation and Work Act defines the organisation, operation and management of police, the specificities of the labour relations of employees within the police and their rights and obligations. It was adopted in 2013 and has been renovated seven times, practically every year. We have before us the eighth amendment to the basic law, which is quite extensive. However, the solutions proposed are designed in particular in the direction of more effective police action. Among all the innovations brought by the proposed amendment to the Law on Organising and Working in the Police, which have been reduced in sixteen areas, I will stress three key areas. Even earlier, it shows the financial implications of EUR 16.5 million of additional expenditure at the annual level in the amendments to the five articles. Therefore, the first amendment, the more effective exercise of supervisory activities by officials, supervisors of the Ministry of the Interior over the police and the relationship between the police and the State Prosecutor's Office. Under the law, the Minister directs the police with written guidelines and instructions, with a medium-term work plan, annual guidelines and individual guidelines where immediate action is needed. However, with regard to individual guidelines, it is particularly specified that the Minister does not have legal powers of direction if the case has already been taken over by the Attorney General. Following the current legislation, it is considered that the State Prosecutor has taken over the direction of police work in the pre-trial procedure from the moment he was informed of the crime. In the next five days, he may provide the police with written instructions for further action. If the Attorney General does not do so, it is considered that he will not direct the work of the police. The proposed amendment removes the situation in which the prosecutor was informed of the crime but did not take the lead. In such a case, it was not possible to direct the Minister. This amendment does not alter or extend the powers of the Minister, but merely regulates the supervision of the work of police officers even in cases where the Prosecutor's Office does not take the lead. The second amendment I am pointing out concerns the National Investigation Office. This police organisation has more effective and effective detection and prosecution of the worst forms of economic crime, corruption and other serious forms of crime. Insinuating that the current government wants to abolish the National Investigation Office in order to destroy its autonomy and thus fully subjugate the police, they are completely unrealistic. The proposal includes a dictatorship that is unequivocal. The National Investigation Office is autonomous in the investigation of criminal offences. It complements, however, the text concerning the internal act by which it is determined which investigations into suspected offences will be undertaken by the Office. Following the current regulation, this act shall be adopted by the Director-General of the Police on a proposal from the Director of the National Investigation Office. The change is foreseen. From now on, there will be two candidates, the Director of the National Investigation Office and the Director of the Directorate of Criminal Police. Whether adding decision-making levels to a highly hierarchical organisational structure such as the police will help to make the work more operational will only show up. In the DeSUS Group, we are currently assessing that such a resolution is appropriate because the autonomy of any body does not constitute absolute autonomy and independence. In the system of parliamentary democracy, each body must be exposed and exposed to the interaction of brakes and balances. As a third non-significant change in the Desus Group, we stress the possibility of identifying the same title and thus the same wage class in case of the reactivation of former police officers. The fact is, the police are facing a negative fluctuation of personnel. Cops and cops aren't enough to count. In the age structure, the increase in the average age is observed. In the last period, however, there has been an overburden of police officers in general. This also increases the possibility of unintentionally causing errors in all types of procedures. At the end of 2009, the possibility of re-employment of retired police officers for the purpose of human resources reinforcements with people with expertise was adopted. This time it is also possible to reintegrate those who left the police for other reasons into the same title and salary class as before. The financial impact of this proposal is estimated at only EUR 12 000 per year, based on the dynamics of such jobs over the past period. The proposer certainly knows what he is proposing, so this information does not give rise to any particularly tangible effects. The proposal includes a number of other provisions: with regard to the posting of attachés abroad, with regard to candidates for trainee officers, auxiliary police, the procedure for obtaining positions in certain positions in the police on the basis of the career system of progress, with regard to the possibility of an allowance for increased work, with regard to the prohibition of membership in political parties and the prohibition of strike in cases of natural and other accidents, in crisis, war or state of emergency, and many other provisions. In fact, the novelty is extremely extensive. However, it is important that the proposal still preserves the basic purpose of the law. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! We live in a openly aggressive time when sources of security threats are spreading and accelerating. This situation must be taken into account by the State authorities and taken actively into account in order to limit them, so measures in the interests of the security of citizens cannot have an alternative. This is also the main motive that Members of the Desus Group are going to vote in favour of assessing that the proposal for a law on police organisation and work is suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.

Furthermore, by supplementing Article 62, the medical commission may, at the first hearing of a police officer, assess that the latter, due to mental health problems, is temporarily unable to exercise police powers where the measure may for the first time last for a maximum period of 60 days. It is right to provide them with appropriate professional psychological assistance, such as established practice abroad. In the Slovenian National Party, we welcome the fact that when former police officers are reinstated in the police, the possibility of being appointed to the same level of title and classified in the same class of payment in which the former police officer was assigned before the end of the employment relationship. Police officers must have the full support of their superiors in carrying out their tasks, as this will make them much more effective on the ground. We have observed that this is a materially comparable solution, as provided for by Article 59 of the Service Act in the Slovenian Army. We believe that hard work requires a fair payment, which they certainly deserve, because they work for the safety of our citizens and citizens. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u298	Thank you very much, Vice President, for your word. Honourable Members, Members of Parliament, the rest of you are invited! Introductoryly, as representatives of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia as proposing a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Road Traffic Rules Act under the first paragraph of Article 138 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, we propose that a third consideration of the draft law be made at this meeting of the National Assembly. Slovenia is one of the European countries that has managed to reduce the number of deaths in traffic accidents from 335 in 1999 to 102 in 2019 through a wider social effort in the last 20 years. At the end of April 2021, the European Commission published a preliminary report on road fatalities for 2020. An estimated 18,000 800 people died in traffic accidents in 2020, 17% less than in 2019. Almost 4,000 people lost their lives on roads in the European Union less than in 2019. The lower volume of traffic as a result of the covid-19 pandemic was evident, although according to the European Commission it had an unmeasured impact on the number of road fatalities. The European Commission has highlighted Slovenia as a country between Member States where at least fatalities have been recorded and the highest percentage of reductions. The European Commission also notes that 18 Member States recorded the lowest number of road deaths in 2020. The number of deaths per million people across the European Union decreased by an average of 17% compared to 2019. The maximum reduction of 20% or more by Slovenia, i.e. at the level minus 42, is still recorded in Belgium, Bulgaria, Denmark, Spain, France, Croatia, Italy and Hungary and Malta. Despite the pandemic, five Member States noted an increase in the number of deaths. Despite the sightings of traffic experts this year, we are still able to contain this trend somehow. According to statistics, 47 people died in traffic accidents from 1 January 2021 to 30 June 2021 and 45 in the same period last year. Any road traffic participant wishes to have a transport system that meets his needs and expectations. The quality of life depends to a large extent also on safe mobility, and therefore road transport is also the responsibility of a country which has the necessary levers, with which it directly or indirectly affects this area in addition to an individual. The main objectives of road safety policy in the country are also pursued by the proposed amendments to the Road Traffic Rules Act, by stricter treatment of drivers who do not take into account the importance of special light and sound signs placed on vehicles with an escort and accompaniment advantage; by stricter treatment of drivers who connect to a vehicle with an advantage; by stricter treatment of drivers who use devices that reduce hearing or visual perception during driving; by setting a suitable lateral distance of 1.5 metres in overcoming bicycles, light motor vehicles and moped drivers; by establishing rules of conduct in the area of common traffic and on surfaces for pedestrians and cyclists; by setting conditions for the participation of light motor vehicles in road traffic; by setting rules for the handling of red lights and traffic signs for the rearing of traffic and by driving on the right. These are key legislative changes that follow the trend in transport and respond to the most acute problems and changes. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u300	Your Honor! At its 30th meeting, on 17 June 2021, the Committee on Infrastructure, Environment and Space considered the draft law amending and supplementing the Law on Road Traffic Rules submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for its consideration and decision following a regular procedure. In the introduction to the explanatory memorandum, the State Secretary of the Ministry of Infrastructure, Aleš Mihelič, explained on behalf of the applicant that the proposal amends or complements certain legal provisions, thus pursuing the main objectives of the national transport policy. In its written opinion, Ms Macek Gustin, the representative of the Law and Law Service, explained that she had pointed out the inconsistencies in the text of the individual articles of the bill, in particular in terms of linguistic clarity, nomotechnical adequacy, under-defined conditions for the participation of special vehicles in road transport, some inconsistencies between the explanation and the wording of the article and the inconsistent application of the provisions of the Road Traffic Rules Act in similar cases. In particular, it highlighted the problem of compliance with Articles 2 and 8 of the draft law with the Constitution and the law on offences. Although the amendments tabled by the coalition groups have removed most of the concerns contained in the written opinion of the SPS, they remain controversial paragraphs 2 and 8 and therefore the representative of the SPS further presented the comments made by the SPS to the said articles. Representative of the State Council mag. Igor Velov stressed that the State Council supports and supports the proposed legal solutions that change and complement transport rules with the aim of enhancing the safety of all road users. The representative of the Transport Safety Agency of the Republic of Slovenia, Ana Marija Hren, stressed that they supported the bill. As the advantages of the area in the draft law, it highlighted stricter sanctions for the use of mobile phones during driving and the use of e-scrolls. In the debate, members of the coalition parliamentary groups stressed their support for the bill because it is extensive, well-prepared and in terms of road safety. It covers the appropriate solutions, changes and additions it brings, which are professionally justified and will help to improve the safety of all road users. The prescribed penalties laid down in the draft law are appropriate, and the proposed fines for offences to exceed the speed limit are entirely comparable to the fines prescribed in Austria for such offences. The new rule, this is the right-hand driving on the traffic light, which has been added to the traffic sign on the right by the red light at the traffic light, if the direction is free, have been marked as a useful measure that will undoubtedly help to improve road traffic flow. They also welcomed the proposed solution to increase penalties for the use of mobile phones while driving. On the other hand, members of the opposition parliamentary groups stressed that they did not support the bill. They warned that the bill introduces a situation leading to an unconstitutional situation when they are in conflict with the Law on Offences, which is a system law, and the Law on Road Traffic Rules. They pointed out that the amendments introduced in Articles 2 and 8 of the bill, the court and the criminal authorities would be in the process of deciding on the dilemma which the standard actually applied. In their view, it is overlooked that the Law on Offences is a statutory law and that it defines general assumptions for determining criminal liability. They also stressed that they do not agree with the proposed solution in Article 16 of the bill, which provides for a reduction in speeding fines, as this solution will lead to a reduction in road safety standards and an increase in the number of such offences. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted a larger number of amendments of the coalition parliamentary groups and the amendment of the SNS Group to Article 19. ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u306	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear Secretary of State with co-workers, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! The proposed Road Traffic Rules Act amends and complements transport rules with the aim of enhancing the safety of all road users. The Ministry of Infrastructure has drawn up a proposal for a law in the context of coordination with a number of stakeholders and has also included proposals from local communities, the Public Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for Transport Safety and other sector ministries. As one of the important instruments of greater traffic safety, the law proposes a stricter handling of drivers during driving, which reduces the hearing or visual perception or ability to control a vehicle. In the context of this change, the use of mobile phones while driving will be much more severely sanctioned. The bill also highlights the stricter treatment of drivers who do not take into account the importance of the special lighting and sound signs that vehicles have with advantage and the stricter treatment of drivers who join the vehicle with or even overtake it. Given that more and more bikers have been involved in the market in recent years, the law provides for an appropriate lateral distance of 1.5 metres in the case of overcoming bikers, light motorcycles and moped drivers whose design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. The conditions for the participation of light motor vehicles in road traffic, including wheelchairs and skippers and electric rollers, shall also be laid down. It is suggested that they may be driven by persons over 14 years of age who are required to ride on a bicycle route or on the right side of a road in a village where the speed is limited to 50 kilometers per hour. A proposal will be made to speed up traffic at the traffic crossing, and a proposal will be made to allow right turns at the red light on the traffic light if it is added a traffic sign for driving at the red light and if the direction is free. In addition to measures that pursue the basic objectives of the national transport policy, the draft law also regulates the liability of the owner or holder of the right to use the vehicle in the event of a service of the offence in such a way as to legitimize the alleged liability of the owner of the vehicle or the holder of the vehicle for the offence committed if the identity of the offender cannot be established. Finally, the bill reduces some fines for lower speed limits in the area of calmed traffic, pedestrian areas, in the area of common traffic, the area of limited speed on the road in the village, on the road outside the settlement, and on the highway and the fast road. It was found that the very amount of the penalty did not affect traffic safety. Substantial greater emphasis should be placed on education, prevention and safe traffic behaviour. I am convinced that all Members here in this Chamber are united, that the number of deaths and seriously injured in traffic accidents must be reduced. In Novi Slovenije – Christian Democrats we advocate the view that transport security must cover the weakest in traffic. These are mainly pedestrians, cyclists and disabled persons, so it is necessary to accelerate the construction and regulation of cycling and pedestrians in particular and to remove architectural and communication barriers in public places. We are pleased to note that there has been a lot of work going on in this area lately. Special attention should be paid to raising children in particular in terms of learning the rules of road transport, behaviour in transport and targeting them on a healthy way of mobility. Members of New Slovenia – Christian Democrats will be unanimously supported by the Road Traffic Rules Act. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u307	Thank you very much. The position of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre will be presented by Matej Udovč. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-07-08-SDZ8-Redna-24.ana.u308	Your Honor! In the context of this item of the agenda, we are deciding on a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Road Traffic Rules Act, which amends and complements certain legal provisions, following efforts to ensure higher traffic safety. In the light of higher traffic safety, the proposal for a law governs certain existing relations between offences and fines. It prepares the legal basis for the use of modern vehicle types, extends the powers, authorised persons and amends certain other restrictions applicable in our road transport system. The adoption of this proposal will finally regulate the conditions for the participation of light motor vehicles in road transport. The text of the bill defines such vehicles as motor-powered vehicles not exceeding 80 centimeters and whose design speed does not exceed 25 kilometres per hour. These vehicles are particularly exposed to electric skiers due to the proliferation of their use. The document that we decide on is understood as a response to the need for regulation of legislation, which will, after a new one, clearly define the conditions for the participation of these vehicles in road transport and specify the rules for their application. The riders of light motor vehicles will have to ride on a bicycle lane, a cycling track or a cycling path, where these traffic surfaces will not be or will not be transportable, they will be allowed to drive at the right time of the road, but only in the traffic area of the settlement. We welcome the proposal for a law in the modern centre Party Group, which also provides for stricter treatment of the use of certain road transport devices. These include devices that have a negative impact on the hearing or visual perception of drivers and thereby reduce their ability to control the vehicle. We believe that laws in force in road transport need to be updated regularly. Finally, not only are we driven by modern logistics trends, but also by technological updates that have a direct impact on the behaviour of road users. The rules must follow these trends in a way that enables an individual to do what is effective logistics, but cannot be compensated in any way by reducing the overall level of safety of road users. The document we have before us also regulates discrepancies in the settlement of court fees in cases of offences. These, in the past, have caused the overall cost of arresting the penalty to vary according to the ratio between the driver and the owner of a vehicle. In this opinion, it is not only an unnecessary distinction in the treatment of participants, but rather an action contrary to the modern trends in road transport, which go in the direction of the so-called Sharing Car or the shared use of vehicles between users. We therefore advocate that road users receive equal treatment and that those who operate vehicles owned by another owner do not have additional costs in the case of fines received, costs in the form of judicial fees. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we assess that the bill contains prudent solutions that approach modern road traffic problems in an appropriate way. We are pleased that the amendments and additions made by the law are based on the opinion of the profession, as this further strengthens our belief that its founders have really followed the central objective of ensuring greater safety for all road users. On the basis of these facts, we will unanimously support the proposal of the Modern Centre Party's parliamentary group. To conclude, where is the evidence that disasters will prevent higher fines? Thank you for your attention.
